It is similar to child abuse, which is also punishable in Austria if it takes place in Thailand. If someone is active abroad as a fighter and comes back to Austria, he is a security threat and then the person in Austria has to answer for the Austrian courts. There are different legal possibilities. The title of asylum seekers can, of course, be recognized, and that is a good thing. Of course, for people who are already born here and are Austrian citizens, we are doing much harder, but of course there must be criminal consequences. ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e6272	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Foreign Minister! Since 1968, there has been the Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty. In this Treaty, the non-nuclear states refrain from acquiring nuclear weapons. In return, the nuclear weapons states have promised to agree on a process of complete destruction of their own arsenals. Nearly 50 years have passed since the signing of the nuclear ceasefire treaty, 25 years since the end of the Cold War, 69 years since the terrible explosions in Hiroshima and Nagasaki – and the nuclear weapons states have still not fulfilled their part of the Treaty, nuclear weapons stocks are still being modernised and not destroyed, nuclear deterrence is still part of NATO doctrine and the nuclear forces still have around 2,000 nuclear missiles ready to fire in a few minutes. The danger of a nuclear explosion, either caused by the state or by terrorists, deliberately or by accident, has not been reduced, but rather increased. Norway, Mexico and Austria have now jointly launched a new initiative, which is considered to be extremely important internationally. There are conferences, above all, on the humanitarian consequences of the use of nuclear weapons, which have found huge advocacy. They are not only publicly clarifying the huge dangers worldwide, but they are also missing a new dynamic, especially with the help of civil society, thereby increasing the pressure on the nuclear weapons states to finally move. This new momentum and this new pressure must be preserved. It is therefore also so important that this conference, which will take place in Vienna in December, will be a success. It is important that Parliament sends a clear signal and, if possible, a unanimous sign. This makes it clear that the government, if it is committed to this area, which has full support for Parliament, and we support our competent diplomats, who are doing excellent work, with our shared strength and energy. I believe that this can do a lot internationally. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e7692	Mrs Mag. Dr. Karl. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e7713	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The performance of the ORF of € 8 million annually for the Austrian film is nothing new. Through the 2011 Film/TV Agreement, the ORF has already supported the production of Austrian cinema films and has already provided €8 million annually from 2011 to 2013. Thus, a truly successful cooperation between the ORF and the Austrian Film Institute was continued since 1981. Now the ORF is to be legally obliged to further promote Austrian film productions or international co-productions with local participation by means of the present amendment of the ORF Act. The target therefore continues to be €8 million per year. However, if the ORF contributed less than €8 million to the Film/Television Agreement in a year, then GIS would be obliged to keep the difference from the broadcasting fee and transfer it to a blocked account. In this way, the ORF is simply motivated to continue to contribute to the production of Austrian films and thus to the success of the film/TV agreement. The fact that it is worth promoting Austrian film productions or international co-productions with local participation is in fact beyond doubt in the face of Austrian film success. We were able to celebrate the greatest success of an Austrian film in 2008 when the film “Die Fälscher” by director Stefan Ruzowitzky was awarded an Oscar as the best foreign-language film. The highest award of the world-renowned film festival in Cannes won Austrian director Michael Haneke in 2009. He got the Golden Palm for his masterpiece "The White Band". In addition, the film won more prizes. Many other examples of the success of Austrian films were mentioned. However, it must also be taken into account that Austrian film productions have a great economic added value. The entire creative economy accounts for 4 percent of GDP in Austria. It is therefore very welcome that, to support the Austrian film industry in the successful implementation of national and international film productions, the Film Location Act, which was passed on 13 March, was recently adopted. ==References====External links== Thus, the existing funding programme “Film Location Austria” has been legally anchored since 2010. As already stated in the materials on the Film Location Act, due to the long preliminary periods in the implementation of film productions, planning security regarding the financing of productions is an essential factor for the sustainable development of Austria as a film production location. Just as the Film Location Act is intended to further improve the framework conditions for film promotion in Austria and to ensure planning security for the affected industry by anchoring an important additional pillar in the Austrian film promotion system, the current amendment of the ORF Act will also contribute to improving the Austrian film funding landscape and film economy. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e7859	Mr President. Mr. Minister! We are here about to solve a problem that should not exist for the ORF at all. Without the contribution to the film/TV agreement, which the ORF apparently does not provide voluntarily, there would not be any such productions at all. This gives the impression that the ORF is a funding station. However, the ORF is not a hub for funding, but a public-law media house, and promotions are not an instrument for content design. In any case, the ORF is inappropriate as a resolution agency because its task to design the program according to the logic of a modern media house does not necessarily have to meet the funding criteria. They thus awaken all kinds of desires. comprehensible demands will come from all possible sides: Sports, religion, culture can register with the same logic the right to mediate and promote – unless they are explicitly taken into account in the ORF law, such as the men's football in § 4b Abs. Point 2 of the ORF Act. What happens in the ORF is not to be regulated in the ORF Act. The ORF Act should only be a guideline for the public service contract. Accordingly, the right place for a debate on content is not the Constitutional Committee or Parliament, but a body for public value. This is the real problem: the link between politics and ORF. No one notices it, because everyone is already used to it, but politics – and actually we are doing it now – is constantly taking control of the ORF. With great effort a Stiftungsrat will become, a public council will be party-politically colored, and this will only be in red and black, although this does not reflect the current majority relations here in Parliament at all. [[Abg. Rädler: (with spread arms pointing left and right) Hello!]] It is bad enough to try to control the ORF from the top and not release the ORF into self-responsibleness, and if it does not detect, even laws such as we now want to decide – or not to decide – will be taken into account. The fact that the ORF doesn't want to add to it is also comprehensible. The €8 million was linked to the fee-refunding, and if there is no more refunding, then there is no longer a mandatory contribution to the film/TV agreement. This link may be politically desirable, but it does not correspond to the idea – our idea – of a public-law media house. We want a modern media house that can also use modern instruments – social media, applications – we want the ORF to be self-responsible, which means, among other things, that it has the necessary flexibility to reduce its infrastructure and staff status if it is no longer modern, and we want, above all, the ORF, within the framework of its public-law contract, to develop the programme independently. The privilege of collecting fees is not a fixed size for us either. Accordingly, we could finally consider setting up an opt-out model for the GIS. Summary: The issue of promoting film and television production is a question that needs to be resolved satisfactorily and with planning horizon. A political commitment for the ORF is, however, the wrong path if the right goal is to be achieved. In any case, we reject any interference of politics into the ORF's operational business on the way to legislation.

However, companies that are considered not to be self-sustainable are at risk of losing the transfer service they receive, the increased family support. In my opinion, this limitation in the permeability of the third to the first labour market and the concern for the loss of this family allowance are a significant obstacle to these people. Although they want to bring along and work an enormous capacity, to do their job and invest their strength, many then do not trust themselves. The purpose of the present legislative amendment is to clarify the fact that family support can be revived even after a failed work attempt. Like our disabled spokesperson Ulli Königsberger-Ludwig said yesterday at the novice of the Federal Disability Act on the point of repayment of the orphan pension: Work increases self-esteem and self-determination, and the task of a policy for people with disabilities must be to support these people as much as possible. ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e12303	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The present Federal Government Agreement, which amends the existing agreement on the development of full-day school forms, does indeed perform several functions, as we know. It is a matter of continuing to force the rapid expansion of all-day school forms so that schoolchildren can not be left to themselves, TV or PlayStation in the afternoon, but play together in a pedagogical manner, learn together, eat together. At the same time, however, an important contribution is made to the compatibility of work and family for both parents. However, this amended agreement still has an effect, namely that the consolidation contribution, which the Minister of Finance also requires of the Federal Ministry of Education, can be applied without having to save in the classroom – that was to me, that was to you all, that was a great concern to us all – and that the total amount for all-day school forms remains the same. You know the details of the agreement. This is a shift in resources, but the total remains the same. This also enables us to further increase the range of all day school forms. Much has already been said about freedom of choice et cetera. I mean, For freedom of choice, we always need a choice, and this is exactly what we want to create together. – Many thanks to the Minister for this successful agreement!

Point Report of the Justice Committee on the Government Template (180 d.a._Other Organiser Federal Act amending the Rules of Procedure, the Enforcement Fees Act, the Law on the Law on Jurisdictions, the Law on Court Fees and the Insolvency (EO-Nov. 2014) (202 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e13450	We're now on the 9th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Magistrate has spoken on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). It's a kilnauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e13477	Mr President. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Appreciate Seers and Advisors! The Rules of Procedure regulate the Enforcement Procedure, i.e. the compulsory enforcement of an obligation, e.g. judicial pledges. A further reform in the field of execution law has been achieved with the present amendment of the Rules of Procedure. At the same time, the Enforcement Fees Act, the Law on Jurisdictions, the Law on Court Fees and the Insolvency Regulations are amended. It is an adjustment that takes into account, on the one hand, suggestions from practice and, on the other, the jurisdiction of the European Court of Human Rights. I would like to highlight the five main points of this amendment in a very short time. On the one hand, we strengthen the rights of incompetent persons in forced auction procedures. An example of the previous system of execution: If the home of a non-processable debtor was auctioned, the supplement could no longer be challenged. With a view to the particular need for protection of non-processive persons, it is therefore appropriate to take due account of the interests of the acquirer of housing into account, subject to certain conditions, to the withdrawal of the surcharge. Secondly, the procedure for the suspension of the execution of legal hearing for the operating creditor is to be followed, and the recurrency procedure should also be re-designed on two sides. Thirdly, this novel of the order of execution means improvements in disputes about the maintenance of children. In the case of disputes relating to executional objections to maintenance claims or to the executional authorisation, the court in which the execution was requested shall decide on the current legal situation. The Court of First Instance shall, in the so-called disputed proceedings, decide by a judgment of the Court of First Instance. However, as there can often be a re-evaluation of the maintenance in this context, it is more appropriate that in maintenance cases, the court is always negotiating and deciding on executional opposition, which is responsible for the concrete procedure. This means that in future, in the event of child support, a lawyer will decide in the proceedings, except for disputes. In any case, this will increase the focus on family law. Finally, these are also amendments to the Enforcement Fees Act. This amendment provides for a slight increase in individual remunerations of the executioners, which is because those remunerations are a part of the salary and have not been adjusted in recent years, which is why a change was necessary here. This will certainly ensure the quality of the executioners and the work of the executioners. These compensations are offset by the increase in the fees for execution. At the same time, however, I would also like to stress that this increase is an index adjustment and that the increase does not go beyond that. These charges are intended to cover the respective performance, namely the introduction of a superior performance, which is certainly a good and fair solution. Fifthly, we take measures against undue bidder agreements in the event of forced auctions. That was a concrete suggestion from the judiciary. Inadmissible bidder agreements are to be terminated in the event of forced auctions by this new regime. The amendment provides that future judges may impose a fine of up to €10,000 if there are agreements that potential bidders do not appear, for example, in auctions or prior agreement that only up to a certain amount will be increased. Ladies and gentlemen! To sum up, we have achieved an important reform step with the change in the system of execution. In addition, I am pleased that this novelle will receive broad support. This is, I think, a good sign of cooperation and especially in the field of justice.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e13536	Next speaker: Mrs Mag. Mulder. Please, ladies and gentlemen.

The leitmotif of this execution order-novelle was to improve the efficiency of demand recovery and, in certain areas, legal protection and to implement two judgments of the European Court of Human Rights against the Republic of Austria. My colleague, Mr Ofenauer, has already explained important points at a glance, and I would like to stress two areas which I particularly welcome as a housing speaker in my party: On the one hand, a clear violation of the European Convention on Human Rights will be fixed in respect of the decision of the European Court of Human Rights in the Zehentner case against Austria. This is about this auction case, which has already been mentioned, that, under certain conditions, the required party will also be able to cancel the contract if an apartment is auctioned by non-processive persons. – I think this is a very, very important point to be repaired here. From the point of view of my political activity, I also feel that the measures against undue bidder agreements in the case of property auctions are important. The judge already has certain possibilities here: to determine the auction date to lead the auction. He is empowered to keep unauthorised appointments, intimidations and so on. But there are also agreements that take place during the auction, and it is not to be underestimated that these bidder agreements are very often also detrimental to the obliged and the creditors. The above-mentioned regulation, which will be valid in the future, provides that agreements made during the auction will be invalid and that there will also be regulatory penalties up to €10 000 in the future or that they will be possible. I think this is a very good novel, which will bring improvements in many areas, and we will be very happy to agree with it. Thank you.

Today, we strengthen the rights of incompetent persons in the execution process. What was the occasion? – The occasion was a woman who was mentally impaired, who was ill and had debt, namely debt of about €7 000 with an installer, and for this reason her apartment was auctioned and she was forced to be cleared. The impact on those affected, who could not at all “define” themselves – under quotation marks – was that she lost her apartment. This person then went to the European Court of Human Rights, and he then transferred the rights of the unqualified persons to the European Court of Human Rights. strengthens that he said, of course, these persons need rights in a case where they later show that they were unable to obtain their legal transactions. This is what Parliament is implementing today, by strengthening the rights of these persons, which are now clear: If people are unable to do so, they can also get a delay later in the execution procedure and later achieve a withdrawal of the award in the event of a forced auction. – Sounds dry, but has important and practical effects for those affected. Minister! The work on the execution procedure, however, was also an occasion for me to look, out of interest, at how many execution procedures there are in Austria, and I was, honestly, surprised by the number. When we talk about demand executions, it was 681,000 in 2013 – that is, just over 3,000 requests for demand execution per day – and if we start from driving, it was over 800 000 and almost 4,000 applications per day. This figure already shows that there is obviously a massive debt problem in Austria, and in this context we should look at the rules on private bankruptcy together – that has been discussed for a long time – because failure, whether in the form of self-employment or also very often a reason for overdebtedness, should not mean that this is an end to economic disponification. There should also be a restart after a failure. In that respect, on the occasion of these figures – and this has nothing to do with the master's proposal – my proposal would be to look at the private bankruptcy and to consider how we can allow people who fail economically to reboot. - Thank you.

Point Report of the Committee of Justice on the Government template (181 d.e._Other Organiser Bundesgesetz, with which the Criminal Procedure Act 1975, the Jugendgerichtsgesetz 1988, the Addictions Act, the Procuratorships Act, the Schöfengesetz 1990, the Reimbursementsgesetz 1972 and the Fellsrechtsgesetz 2014 are amended (203 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e13931	We're on our way to the 10th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Magistrate has spoken on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Steinhauser. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e13958	Ladies and gentlemen! We are discussing today a reform of the criminal code. It is a variety of measures proposed in this law. One has to go through the proposals step by step, so to speak, if one makes an assessment of these new laws. The first point is a three-year period for prosecution investigations. The keyword for this three-year period is: “long investigation” – we know it from the newspaper, I don’t count it all. I am saying that the three-year period, Mr Minister, is not wrong, but it is a little placebo because it will not change the basic problems. Without this, most proceedings will end or will be charged before the three-year period. In fact, the three-year period is limited to those procedures which result from the complexity of the public prosecutor's work, but precisely in these proceedings it is possible to determine beyond the three-year period. That is why the three-year period will not change. Above all, the basic problem of the long duration of the procedure lies quite elsewhere. The basic problem is that the duration of the procedure is due to the fact that there are legal requirements that there are resources and that a single procedure entails a specific effort. The three-year period will not change any of these three parameters. So if we want to shorten the duration of the procedure, we have to start concretely with those areas that are going to extend the duration of the procedures. What's that? – Tag, for example, reporting obligation: The prosecutors wrote to the 5,000 reports in 2013. It takes time. It's going through ten stations. ParlaMint-AT_2014-07-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e14133	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! Appreciate fellow citizens! “The practice is not only at the end, but already at the beginning of the theory.” This quote by Herbert Marcuse, a German-US philosopher, illustrates the course of our Criminal Procedure Change Act 2014. [[Abg. Pirklhuber: That you quote Marcus, we are pleased!]] We want to add experience from the practice of this law – we implemented a century reform in criminal trial law in 2008 and we have criminal trial law into the 21st century. ==References== It is now more important to round off what has, in practice, opened up and is indicated to be changed in these years. Last June, the Federal Government was invited by the National Council to present the Government's proposal, and I can now briefly lead you through the essential points. The previous version of the criminal proceedings has chosen a very wide-ranging accusation for legal protection reasons. In general, when a person is accused, a pre-condemnification is often carried out, even in the case of non-substantial ads. If they become publicly known, someone will be labeled guilty very quickly. Therefore, this novelle now separates the term “accusing person ” from the term“ suspect. ” "The accused" is now the one who is suspicious of the fact and is found to be guilty of this suspicion, otherwise, on the appropriate initial suspicion, we only speak of a "suspect". In any event, I would also like to point out that the extent of legal protection is the same for both the suspect and the accused. Another main point of the presentation is the reduction in the duration of the proceedings, which is now set by the judicial review of the maximum duration of the investigation procedure for three years after which the courts are to be examined. This serves to control the work of prosecutors and criminal police, but also gives the investigators appropriate means of printing themselves, and it avoids the situation of the accused. The speeding-up of proceedings is also a further change, namely the second professional judge in court proceedings, which we are now introducing again, especially in the case of serious offences against the person, but also in the case of serious offences of economic crime and corruption. Thirdly, there is the new mandate procedure which has already been discussed. There has already been a mandate procedure by 1999, but this mandate procedure, as we want to implement it today, has far higher legal standards. The absence of a main proceedings and the adoption of a judicial penalty require three conditions: First of all, the question of debt and punishment must be clarified, the defendant must have already been heard and, in particular, sufficient information must be ensured that the main proceedings do not have to be negotiated. In addition, only a fine or a conditional sentence may be imposed. Then there is the opposition against punishment. He can raise both the accused and the victim. I believe that the discussion we had in the Committee on this issue is very important – we will once again highlight these victim rights, which we have strengthened by the amendment, in the debate on a motion for a resolution once again. It is also about the equality of arms between charges and defence in the field of evidence of experts, where the accused now also has the right to file an application for withdrawal in the event of partiality or reasonable doubt on the subject matter of the expert and also to propose a person as an expert. Furthermore, we have the point that we double the amounts the acquitted by the Republic as a substitute for the defense costs. Mr Steinhauser, I know your arguments, but I would also like to point out the following: It is not just the budgetary situation that prevents us from fully ensuring that, but I think there is a big difference. You, as a good lawyer, when I met you, know that there is a difference between criminal and civil trial and that it is impossible to compare the acquitted with the inferior or obscene in the civil trial. Consider what that would mean! In the logical consequence of your derivation, we would have to ask the defendant and then the convicts to pay full costs against the Republic! Therefore, I believe that the solution we have found is a really good and supportive solution even in our budgetary difficult times. I expressly thank you for the good, constructive and objective climate in the Committee on Justice. This debate should continue. I would like to say that the adoption of this proposal will, in any event, improve access to the law and speed up the procedures while respecting the legitimate interests of the parties involved and thus strengthen our rule of law again. - Thank you.

Overlong procedures are EMRK-unfavourable, and therefore we are in duty here – but not only from the EMRK, but also from the High House. In July 2013, and in this resolution, my predecessor was invited to submit to the National Council, as far as possible, appropriate legal plans for the completion of the reform work and for the elimination of currently visible defects, including the demand for judicial control of the duration of the proceedings. That is, what we are doing here with the measures to limit the duration of the proceedings is nothing but following exactly what Parliament has rightly called for, and that cannot be a mistake. I believe that this three-year deadline alone will have a psychological effect. Everyone knows that. And in the end, nothing can happen, because – and this is what is foreseen in the draft – if there are really legitimate reasons for a procedure to take longer, then it will of course be possible, with appropriate judicial control – as required by Parliament in the previous year. But this amendment not only pursues the objective of a procedural acceleration, but also the objective of strengthening the legal protection of the parties involved. What do I mean by that? . Now I come to speak of what Mr Stefan has already mentioned: the differentiation between the mere suspect and the accused. Only the defendant should, on account of a more concrete suspicion, then are actually entered in the register. That is, I think, an important step, which, as I have taken many conversations with journalists, is also taken positively from their side, because simply the status as suspects is not saying anything at all, and that will be the case. And only as an accused person is you in a position where you have to say that there is at least once a concrete initial suspicion, and before that is not the case. Now, of course, one can be philosophized about the purely semantic meaning of the terms “suspected” and “accused” but I think that in the end the content of this law will also influence the use of the terms in everyday life, and that will be reflected accordingly. I just thought: My God, in common, national language, is often said to be “the usual suspects, ” and that is, according to general understanding, far from being an accused person. – So I think that will happen and this differentiation will prove to be true. One final point concerning the objectivity of the conduct of proceedings as one of the central features of an independent and objective judicial system: Finally, there have been discussions on the independence of experts appointed by the public prosecutor in the preliminary proceedings and which should then also be used by the court in the main proceedings. We have specifically re-examined this point in this novel. And with this novel, we have given the accused the opportunity to influence the choice of the expert. The aim is to significantly improve the objectivity of the appointment of the experts and also the legal status of the accused in this area. I believe that this regulation will also succeed. It has already been mentioned: The reintroduction of the second appeal will serve to speed up the proceedings and also to increase the quality of larger procedures. That is what I think is really important. And now I come to the last point, which was, of course, the most controversial, the mandate procedure. Especially since – I would like to say that again – we have really received many, many suggestions, we have also responded to much of what has come of criticism. We have really taken these concerns into account, including the concerns of the victim protection institutions, by allowing the victims, in the present form, the possibility of torpedoing the mandate procedure at all by an opposition. Finally, I believe that this is a very strong measure in favour of the victims. I have no problem with this either, because I assume that the mandate procedure should, of course, be a method which the judge should choose as a solution only if everything is really clear, even with regard to the rights and claims of the victims. Another small point: Of course, the mandate procedure will ultimately be able to accelerate the procedure. I also do not think that the possibility of disversion is immediately being crossed and is affecting it.
